Your prior knowledge is not evidence and must never be reported\n  as a finding.\n- Every number, date, named milestone, and named threshold must carry an inline\n  citation `[src: <url>]`. If you cannot attach one, delete the claim — do not\n  soften it into a range, an approximation, or a "typically" formulation.\n- Report figures exactly as the source states them, with period and units.\n- If a tool result says the data was not found, that is a negative result.\n  Record it. Naming the formula does not make the output grounded.\n- If two sources conflict, report both with citations and say they conflict.\n\n### Know Your Tool\'s Limits\n- Your search tool returns short snippets and a machine-written summary. It does\n  NOT return document bodies and cannot page through a filing.\n- Do NOT try to keyword-search inside one document with `site:` filters or\n  repeated quoted phrases against the same URL. It will not surface deeper\n  content and wastes your budget. Use it only to decide which\n  underlying result to read; cite only URLs from the results list.\n- Judge relevance before using a result. A result about a different company,\n  filing, or year is not weak evidence — it is no evidence. Discard it.\n- Check each source\'s period against your assigned period. A source outside that\n  window is out of scope, not fresher data. Discard it, and never treat a\n  later-period document as a conflict with an in-period one.\n- Prefer primary sources (filings, company releases, official statistics) over\n  secondary coverage, and secondary over aggregators. Never treat forums,\n  sitemaps, or unrelated filings as sources. A criterion isn\'t exhausted until\n  every plausible assigned tool has been tried on it at least once — not\n  merely until your first-choice tool has failed at it several times.\n- Ending with several criteria each given one honest attempt and an explicit\n  NOT FOUND is a better outcome than exhausting the budget on the first\n  criterion and never reaching the rest.\n\n### Guidelines\n- Every tool call targets your objective, with specific entities, an explicit\n  period, and the metric you are after.\n- Stay in your task scope; do not do other agents\' work.\n- Distinguish what a source states from what you infer. Label inference.\n- Note contradictions and ambiguity rather than smoothing them over.\n\n### Output Format\nUse these sections, in order. Advanced\u202fMicro\u202fDevices’ reported purchases of property and equipment (capital expenditures) have risen steadily over the last three fiscal years.\u202fIn the FY\u202f2025 10‑K filed on\u202fDecember\u202f27\u202f2025, AMD disclosed that it spent approximately **$1.55\u202fbillion** on property and equipment.\u202fThe FY\u202f2024 10‑K filed on\u202fDecember\u202f28\u202f2024 shows purchases of about **$1.32\u202fbillion**, while the FY\u202f2023 10‑K filed on\u202fDecember\u202f31\u202f2023 records purchases of roughly **$1.15\u202fbillion**.\u202fThese figures illustrate a year‑over‑year increase of roughly\u202f34\u202fpercent from FY\u202f2023 to FY\u202f2024 and a further\u202f17\u202fpercent rise from FY\u202f2024 to FY\u202f2025, reflecting AMD’s continued investment in manufacturing capacity, research facilities, and equipment to support its product roadmap.\u202fThe source documents are the AMD FY\u202f2025, FY\u202f2024 and FY\u202f2023 The filing shows that AMD spent **$2,191\u202fmillion** on property and equipment in FY\u202f2024, compared with **$2,424\u202fmillion** in FY\u202f2023. This represents a decline of roughly **9½\u202fpercent** year‑over‑year, indicating that the company modestly slowed its capital‑expenditure program, likely reflecting a combination of completed facility upgrades and a more disciplined approach to new‑plant investments while still supporting its growth initiatives. The lower outflow also helped improve the net cash provided by operating activities, contributing to a stronger overall cash‑flow position in 2024.\u202fSource: AMD FY\u202f2024 Form\u202f10‑K Investors also may use cash flow information to evaluate an issuer’s need for external financing, to determine the reasons for differences between net income and associated cash receipts and payments, and to understand the effects of both its cash and noncash investing and financing transactions on an issuer’s financial position. Accordingly, we remind issuers and auditors to approach the preparation, review, and audit of the statement of cash flows with a focus on quality for the benefit of investors. We remind issuers, auditors, and others of the importance of performing an objective analysis from the perspective of a reasonable investor when evaluating the materiality of both the financial statement and ICFR impacts of an error in the statement of cash flows, including the significance of the statement of cash flows to the investor’s complete understanding of the financial condition of the company.\n**Source**: https://www.sec.gov/news/statement/munter-statement-cash-flows-120423\n\n\n# Result 3\n**Title**: SEC.gov | The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors\n**Content**: Title: SEC.gov | The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors\n# The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors. The statement of cash flows is integral to a complete set of financial statements, and it should therefore be subject to the same level of due professional care, effective internal controls, and robust, high-quality audit as other financial statements. Unfortunately, we have observed that preparers and auditors may not always apply the same rigor and attention to the statement of cash flows as they do to other financial statements, which may impede high quality financial reporting for the benefit of investors. This is evidenced by the statement of cash flows consistently being a leading area of financial statement restatements, and by our observations of material weaknesses in internal control over financial reporting (“ICFR”) around the preparation and presentation of the statement of cash flows. Investors also may use cash flow information to evaluate an issuer’s need for external financing, to determine the reasons for differences between net income and associated cash receipts and payments, and to understand the effects of both its cash and noncash investing and financing transactions on an issuer’s financial position. For these reasons, the statement of cash flows is a critical component of high quality financial reporting for investors. Accordingly, we remind issuers and auditors to approach the preparation, review, and audit of the statement of cash flows with a focus on quality for the benefit of investors. We remind issuers, auditors, and others of the importance of performing an objective analysis from the perspective of a reasonable investor when evaluating the materiality of both the financial statement and ICFR impacts of an error in the statement of cash flows, including the significance of the statement of cash flows to the investor’s complete understanding of the financial condition of the company. We remind auditors that in planning the audit and when evaluating errors, a materiality level is established for the financial statements as a whole and it would not be appropriate for auditors to establish a materiality level for the statement of cash flows that exceeds the materiality level for the financial statements as a whole.\n**Source**: https://www.sec.gov/newsroom/speeches-statements/munter-statement-cash-flows-120423#_ftn18\n\n\n# Result 4\n**Title**: SEC.gov | The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors\n**Content**: Title: SEC.gov | The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors\n# The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors. The statement of cash flows is integral to a complete set of financial statements, and it should therefore be subject to the same level of due professional care, effective internal controls, and robust, high-quality audit as other financial statements. Unfortunately, we have observed that preparers and auditors may not always apply the same rigor and attention to the statement of cash flows as they do to other financial statements, which may impede high quality financial reporting for the benefit of investors. This is evidenced by the statement of cash flows consistently being a leading area of financial statement restatements, and by our observations of material weaknesses in internal control over financial reporting (“ICFR”) around the preparation and presentation of the statement of cash flows. Investors also may use cash flow information to evaluate an issuer’s need for external financing, to determine the reasons for differences between net income and associated cash receipts and payments, and to understand the effects of both its cash and noncash investing and financing transactions on an issuer’s financial position. For these reasons, the statement of cash flows is a critical component of high quality financial reporting for investors. Accordingly, we remind issuers and auditors to approach the preparation, review, and audit of the statement of cash flows with a focus on quality for the benefit of investors. We remind issuers, auditors, and others of the importance of performing an objective analysis from the perspective of a reasonable investor when evaluating the materiality of both the financial statement and ICFR impacts of an error in the statement of cash flows, including the significance of the statement of cash flows to the investor’s complete understanding of the financial condition of the company. We remind auditors that in planning the audit and when evaluating errors, a materiality level is established for the financial statements as a whole and it would not be appropriate for auditors to establish a materiality level for the statement of cash flows that exceeds the materiality level for the financial statements as a whole.\n**Source**: https://www.sec.gov/newsroom/speeches-statements/munter-statement-cash-flows-120423#_ftn4\n\n\n# Result 9\n**Title**: SEC.gov | The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors\n**Content**: Title: SEC.gov | The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors\n# The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors. For these reasons, the statement of cash flows is a critical component of high quality financial reporting for investors. Accordingly, we remind issuers and auditors to approach the preparation, review, and audit of the statement of cash flows with a focus on quality for the benefit of investors. We remind issuers, auditors, and others of the importance of performing an objective analysis from the perspective of a reasonable investor when evaluating the materiality of both the financial statement and ICFR impacts of an error in the statement of cash flows, including the significance of the statement of cash flows to the investor’s complete understanding of the financial condition of the company. In forming its opinion, an auditor evaluates whether the financial statements present fairly, in all material respects, an entity’s financial position, results of operations, and *cash flows* in conformity with the applicable financial reporting framework. We encourage issuers to carefully consider how to best present cash and noncash information, and whether additional information should be disclosed to facilitate an investor’s understanding of the statement of cash flows and the financial statements as a whole. For example, we believe issuers could further disaggregate amounts currently reported in the statement of cash flows, disclose additional information to better enable investors to understand the relationships between amounts reported in the statement of cash flows and those in the statement of financial position, and consider reporting operating cash flows under the direct method. Issuers and auditors have a responsibility, under securities laws and professional standards, to apply the same high level of care and professionalism to the preparation, review, and audit of the statement of cash flows as is required for the other financial statements. Investors should be provided with transparent, meaningful, and high-quality cash flow information that is subject to rigor in both preparation and auditing, consistent with the other components of the financial statements.\n**Source**: https://sec.gov/news/statement/munter-statement-cash-flows-120423\n\n\n# Result 15\n**Title**: SEC.gov | The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors\n**Content**: Title: SEC.gov | The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors\nUnfortunately, we have observed that preparers and auditors may not always apply the same rigor and attention to the statement of cash flows as they do to other financial statements, which may impede high quality financial reporting for the benefit of investors. Synthesized Summary***\n    Advanced\u202fMicro\u202fDevices’ reported purchases of property and equipment (capital expenditures) have risen steadily over the last three fiscal years.\u202fIn the FY\u202f2025 10‑K filed on\u202fDecember\u202f27\u202f2025, AMD disclosed that it spent approximately **$1.55\u202fbillion** on property and equipment.\u202fThe FY\u202f2024 10‑K filed on\u202fDecember\u202f28\u202f2024 shows purchases of about **$1.32\u202fbillion**, while the FY\u202f2023 10‑K filed on\u202fDecember\u202f31\u202f2023 records purchases of roughly **$1.15\u202fbillion**.\u202fThese figures illustrate a year‑over‑year increase of roughly\u202f34\u202fpercent from FY\u202f2023 to FY\u202f2024 and a further\u202f17\u202fpercent rise from FY\u202f2024 to FY\u202f2025, reflecting AMD’s continued investment in manufacturing capacity, research facilities, and equipment to support its product roadmap.\u202fThe source documents are the AMD (xiii)\xa0fuel shortages or interruptions in electricity, transportation systems, labor strikes, work stoppages or other interruptions to, or difficulties in, the employment of labor in the major markets where Toyota purchases materials, components and supplies for the production of its products or where its products are produced, distributed or sold; (xiv)\xa0the impact of natural calamities, epidemics, political and economic instability, fuel shortages or interruptions in social infrastructure, wars, terrorism and labor strikes, including their negative effect on Toyota’s vehicle production and sales; Correspondence Letter. United States Securities and Exchange Commission. We are responding to the comment contained in your letter dated May\xa030, 2007 (the “Comment Letter”) related to the above referenced filing. For your convenience, we have repeated the comment contained in the Comment Letter below in italic type before our response. Form 10-K for the Fiscal Year Ended January\xa031, 2007. Consolidated Financial Statements and Supplementary Data. Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows, page 58. We have historically disclosed sales and maturities in a single line item in our statement of cash flows because we believe that separately disclosing these amounts is not material to our investors. We have based this conclusion on several factors, including:. However, in light of the Staff’s comment, we will in future Form 10-K filings provide separate disclosure of sales and maturities of marketable securities in our consolidated statements of cash flows. The presentation in future Form 10-K filings will be as follows:. In responding to the Staff’s comment, we revisited the underlying detail for these investing activities. We supplementally advise the Staff that we discovered that the total amount of sales and maturities of marketable securities for fiscal 2007 incorrectly included the proceeds from the maturities of investments in cash equivalents. Similarly the purchases of marketable securities incorrectly included purchases of investments in cash equivalents. The reported and adjusted balances were as follows (amounts in thousands):. Notwithstanding these differences, the net amount of purchases and sales and maturities of marketable securities of $128,194,000 remains the same, the net cash used in investing activities remains the same and the differences do not have any impact on the total amount of cash and cash equivalents and short-term and noncurrent marketable securities on our balance sheet. Similarly, it has no effect on any other reported amount in our financial statements. We advise the Staff that we will reflect the adjusted amounts in next year’s 10-K filing. Additionally, in response to your request, we acknowledge that:. We would appreciate the Staff’s confirmation by telephone that we have provided an adequate response to the Staff’s comment. R (FWG.BE) H2 FY2025 earnings call transcript\nRecord EBITDA of $333 million, up 6% from FY24, with improved margin to 9.6% as we continue to target year-on-year margin improvement. The balance sheet is in a strong position as a result of the reduction of our gross debt, and the consistent delivery of cash flow sets a solid foundation to invest in growth projects in FY26 that will deliver upside in FY27 and beyond. Mark has already touched on the divisional performance that contributed to the FY25 results, with the increases in revenue, EBITDA, and EBITDA underpinned by the scale and consistency of our Contract Mining division, complemented by improved performance across drilling services. As Mark has already commented on, the business delivered a record free cash flow of $286.1 million in FY25. The team\'s continued focus on working capital management delivered cash conversion of 99% in the year, with $658.9 million of operating cash flow before interest and tax. It is worth noting on this slide that the negative cash flow in FY22 was the result of increased CapEx investment due to several large project wins, which has assisted in the strong positive cash generation in the subsequent years. Our focus in FY2026 is to further build upon the great performance we have delivered over the last three years and deliver another year of strong free cash flow. Free cash flow generation in FY2026 will enable us to allocate capital to winning work in support of earnings growth into FY2027 and beyond, continue paying dividends and, if appropriate, buy back our shares, and further reduce our gross debt to deliver meaningful interest cost reductions to further support earnings growth and cash generation, in turn creating an even stronger balance sheet. CapEx to be approximately $340 million, an increase on FY25 to support growth, resulting in free cash flow greater than $160 million. It will be similar with our expectation in relation to FY25 from a free cash flow perspective, noting that for the last sort of five years, we have sort of hit sort of greater than 95% cash conversion across the Perenti Group.\n**Source**: https://finance.yahoo.com/quote/FWG.BE/earnings/FWG.BE-H2-2025-earnings_call-318877.html\n\n\n# Result 12\n**Title**: Perenti Ltd. On the company’s earnings call, CFO Colette Kress said total revenue rose to **$68 billion**, up **73%** year-over-year, while the company also posted record operating income and free cash flow. Kress said the quarter’s sequential growth was a record, with NVIDIA adding **$11 billion** in data center revenue versus the prior quarter. Fourth-quarter data center revenue totaled **$62 billion**, up **75%** year-over-year and **22%** sequentially, driven primarily by “sustained strength in Blackwell and the Blackwell Ultra ramp.” She added that even Hopper products and “much of the six-year-old Ampere-based products are sold out in the cloud” due to high demand for NVIDIA infrastructure. She added that analyst expectations for 2026 CapEx among the top five cloud providers and hyperscalers—customers that collectively represent “a little over **50%**” of NVIDIA’s data center revenue—have increased by nearly **$120 billion** since the start of the year and are approaching **$700 billion**. Kress said the company’s sovereign AI business more than tripled year-over-year in fiscal 2026 to **over $30 billion**, driven mainly by customers in Canada, France, the Netherlands, Singapore, and the U.K. On China, Kress said small amounts of H200 products for China-based customers were approved by the U.S. government, but NVIDIA has not generated any revenue and does not know whether imports will be allowed. Kress also said starting this quarter NVIDIA will include stock-based compensation expense in its non-GAAP results. The company’s product portfolio spans discrete GPUs for gaming and professional visualization (marketed under the GeForce and NVIDIA RTX lines), high-performance data center accelerators used for AI training and inference (including widely adopted platforms such as the A100 and H100 series), and Tegra SoCs for automotive and edge applications. Nvidia CFO Colette Kress said the company is “racing to scale supply to meet incredible demand” for its recently launched Blackwell GPUs and associated systems that have been touted to deliver major leaps in generative AI performance and efficiency. Kress made the comments during the AI computing giant’s Wednesday earnings call, where the company reported that its third-quarter revenue nearly doubled to $35.1 billion mainly thanks to continuing high demand for its data center chips and systems. In a prepared commentary on the latest earnings, Kress said Nvidia is scheduled to begin shipping Blackwell products, now in full production, by January, with plans to ramp up those shipments in its 2026 fiscal year, which corresponds with the following months. “Both Hopper and Blackwell systems have certain supply constraints, and the demand for Blackwell is expected to exceed supply for several quarters in fiscal 2026,” she wrote. On the call, Kress added that Nvidia is “on track to exceed” its previous Blackwell revenue estimate of several billion dollars for the fourth quarter, which wraps up at the end of January, as its “visibility into supply continues to increase.”. During the call’s question-and-answer session, a financial analyst asked Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang to address a Sunday report by industry publication The Information, which detailed concerns from a few customers about Blackwell GPUs overheating in the most powerful configurations of its Grace Blackwell GB200 NVL72 rack-scale server platform. In response to the question about The Information’s report on Blackwell GPUs overheating in GB200 NVL72 systems, Huang noted that while Blackwell production is in “full steam” with the company exceeding previous revenue estimates, he said the engineering Nvidia does with OEM and cloud computing partners is “rather complicated.”. Andy Lin, CTO at top Nvidia partner Mark III Systems in Houston, Texas, told CRN that while demand for Nvidia’s data center GPUs and associated systems are “obviously incredibly strong,” it has “set such a high bar” for itself with several quarters of triple-digit growth.\n**Source**: https://www.crn.com/news/components-peripherals/2024/nvidia-we-are-racing-to-scale-supply-to-meet-incredible-blackwell-demand?itc=refresh\n\n\n# Result 13\n**Title**: Nvidia: ‘We Are Racing To Scale Supply To Meet Incredible’ Blackwell Demand\n**Content**: # Nvidia: ‘We Are Racing To Scale Supply To Meet Incredible’ Blackwell Demand. *‘Both Hopper and Blackwell systems have certain supply constraints, and the demand for Blackwell is expected to exceed supply for several quarters in fiscal 2026,’ Nvidia finance chief Colette Kress said in her third-quarter commentary before CEO Jensen Huang addressed issues with Blackwell.*. Investors also may use cash flow information to evaluate an issuer’s need for external financing, to determine the reasons for differences between net income and associated cash receipts and payments, and to understand the effects of both its cash and noncash investing and financing transactions on an issuer’s financial position. We remind issuers, auditors, and others of the importance of performing an objective analysis from the perspective of a reasonable investor when evaluating the materiality of both the financial statement and ICFR impacts of an error in the statement of cash flows, including the significance of the statement of cash flows to the investor’s complete understanding of the financial condition of the company.\n**Source**: https://www.sec.gov/newsroom/speeches-statements/munter-statement-cash-flows-120423#_ftn13\n\n\n# Result 13\n**Title**: SEC.gov | The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors\n**Content**: # The Statement of Cash Flows: Improving the Quality of Cash Flow Information Provided to Investors. For the quarter, GAAP earnings per diluted share was $4.93, up 33% from the previous quarter and up 765% from a year ago. Conflicts\n\nThere is a minor discrepancy regarding the timeline and exact expectations for the $20 billion Sovereign AI revenue milestone:\n*   In the Q2 FY2026 earnings transcript (dated August 27, 2025), management stated: *"We are on track to achieve over $20 billion in Sovereign AI revenue this year, more than double that of last year."* [src: https://www.roic.ai/quote/NVDA:QF/transcripts/2026-year/2-quarter].\n*   Conversely, a secondary news summary covering the same period stated: *"Kress added that Nvidia is \'on track to generate over $20 billion in sovereign AI revenue\' next year, more than double last year’s level"* [src: https://deepnewz.com/europe/nvidia-projects-600-b-2025-data-center-spend-20-b-sovereign-ai-sales-8a28c520] [secondary-sourced].\n*   Ultimately, the Q4 FY2026 transcript highlights resolved this by stating that sovereign AI revenue had grown past both projections, officially tripling year-over-year in FY2026 to exceed $30 billion [src: https://www.defenseworld.net/2026/02/26/nvidia-q4-earnings-call-highlights.html].\n\n---\n\n### Interpretation\n\n*The following analysis is derived from the retrieved financial data and management commentary:*\n\nNVIDIA has positioned itself not merely as a component supplier but as a foundational **AI infrastructure builder**. Because NVIDIA relies on a fabless manufacturing model, its own capital expenditures (purchases of property and equipment) have historically remained highly constrained relative to its explosive revenues. \n\nInstead, the true capital driver in NVIDIA\'s ecosystem is **customer-side CapEx**. Top hyperscalers and sovereign nations are financing massive multi-megawatt facilities, which management describes as "AI Factories." By aligning its product cycles (Hopper, Blackwell, Blackwell Ultra, and the future Rubin) with the $600 billion to $700 billion annual infrastructure budgets of its customers [src: https://www.roic.ai/quote/NVDA:QF/transcripts/2026-year/2-quarter] [src: https://www.defenseworld.net/2026/02/26/nvidia-q4-earnings-call-highlights.html], NVIDIA effectively captures the capital expenditure of the entire global cloud ecosystem. \n\nFurthermore, the expansion of Sovereign AI (countries investing directly in domestic computing power) has emerged as a major capital allocation category, tripling year-over-year to over $30 billion [src: https://www.defenseworld.net/2026/02/26/nvidia-q4-earnings-call-highlights.html]. This shifts capital intensity away from NVIDIA\'s balance sheet while driving the high-margin, full-stack software and networking segments (e.g., SpectrumX, NVLink switch systems) that unify these multi-gigawatt facilities [src: https://www.roic.ai/quote/NVDA:QF/transcripts/2026-year/2-quarter] [src:
